QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #14 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations 17/40 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Hi all, apologies for the lack of posting! Like most of you I have a day job, I try and learn RTM on in the background and learn when I can but it's very sporadic. I feel a lot of things have fallen in to place and when I have the time I will post demo trades and see what everyone thinks. One thing I have been thinking about it is accuracy at which the masters trade. It does confirm that nothing in the market is 'random' to be able to do this. I set myself the task this afternoon to look for a trade in the past that I may have taken using the knowledge from RTM. On top of that I wanted to find the real reason price turned at that exact point. Hopefully the charts below will show you what I have found. My thought process was this; 1. Look for a trade at a flag limit. 2. remember the flag limit is itself a flag so look for the associated FTR. 3. Within the FTR look for the point at which the decision was made to turn price in the opposite direction, which I believe was an FTR in itself. 4. Price returned to the FTR, within the FTR of the FL. A mouth full but makes sense. I'm going to find more of these examples but here is the first; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations 18/40 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Any feedback is much appreciated. EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME I was reading Warrens journal on Friday night. If you haven't done so, I recommend you do as it's an essential read. Know yourself and know who you are. He also has a Nissan GTR, a car my Dad loves and something I will hopefully purchase for him one day. When I trade live I've decided I will only be doing so with a good foundation, at the base of that foundation will be good money management, knowing my loses will be limited to an amount I can psychologically deal with. This will give me great piece of mind which mean the amount I trade will not affect my judgement. The goal is to be a good trader, not to make money, if I'm a good trader the money will come as a byproduct. I liken this to any profession the best people in their field can demand the highest wages! So, I've been going backwards and forwards a lot this week, a couple of phrases sticking in my mind. These aren't exact quotes but here I go ' the space between two FL's is a PAZ', 'I only trade FL's'. There are a few more but I might use them in another post! I believe my journey has only just started, on Friday gone actually, everything before that was just preparation, putting talcum powder on my feet and stocking up on supplies. Few more penny's dropping. Still lots of questions though. This is where I'm at right now; I think if we engulf a FL/RBR/BDR we want to go to the next FL, this could be another DBD/RBR but we would be targeting the RBD/DBR within the DBD/RBR. Or we could be heading for the origin of the whole move down, we could just draw a box around the cap on price and make that our target, or we could look for the source, the FL/FTR, the real beginning of our move up or down. I hope this makes sense. I'm just typing the things running around my head at the minute. Where I am struggling when trying to prove this concept is in the lower time frames. I know it is irrelevant as long as we keep an eye on the bigger picture but it's knowing when to stop, knowing when to say, these are my targets, I know there are more FL's further down the time frames but these are the FL's I'm going to trade. What importance do we put on an FTR that isn't a FL? The FTR to the FL has caught my attention so far. I from what I have seen a reaction should be expected there and it should be trade able as long as you look at the bigger picture. People like to trade the same things in different ways. Dissecting IF's trades really 27/40 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - RTM Community helped me learn a lot, I hope I'm learning the correct things. IF seems to just go straight HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT for the source as long as he has confirmation PA, and he then exits at the source of a move when he has confirmation that source will be the turning point for a move in the opposite direction. I think this is just part of IF's trading plan. Others look at the same area but take the trade after more confirmation. That brings me nicely on to creating my own trading plan, how can I possibly think about becoming a trading without a plan. 1. Money management, the key. What am I willing to risk per trade? 2. What am I going to trade? Just FL's? FTR's? Only certain FTR's. Will I take multiple trades on the what I consider to be one move if I get additional confirmation PA that allows for another entry? 3. How many trades will I have open at any one time? 4. How many pairs/commodities will I monitor? I can't monitor them all, which ones will I chose? etc What I've been looking for are basic rules, rules to follow the market, rules to put on top of my foundation of money management. If I have an engulf, I look for an entry. Looking at the charts this morning I'm realized I need more time in front of the charts to train myself and make this second nature, or close to. I really believe price will go back to the source of a move if an engulf occurs, it's keeping an eye on what else has been engulfed. Anyway, I've rambled far too much, I just had a lot of thoughts I needed to get out of my head. Hopefully at least one person can learn from my struggles, and if I might be heading in the wrong direction please just let me know. In the homework area it's clear we could mark hundreds on one chart, but which ones are the important ones. I've read a lot about swing highs and swing lows, SR and FTRs. When I look at the chart the swing highs and lows are just bouncing off flag limits, so by drawing my SR lines I am just marking of the boundries of the flags in the time frame I'm looking at. Then I have to remember that this high is bounching off something in the past, we know it's going to be a flag limit as this high is the limit of our flag. What i've done is mark the FTR after my highs around my SR lines, because this FTR is my flag limit. Where I sometimes get unstuck is when my FTR after my high/low doesn't actually break the FL keeping the flag down/up. Do I ignore this FTR and give more importance to the FL/FTR which forms above? Firstly I'll add the caveat that I'm no expert and this is just what I believe is happening from what I've learned so far. To be honest, most things have started falling in to place in the last few weeks for me, I started looking at the questions as we already have all of the answers. So first question ' Hi robbie, can you expain more with IF's chart about 'engulf, return to source of move'? and why price 'had hit its target'?' https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 5/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In IF's chart I believe the first blue box is the FLAG, the red box isn't the FL of this FLAG, this is an important point to remember. A FL in the past held that flag up and the break of the FL in the past creates a new FL holding price down. Bearing this in mind, the last blue box is our new FL, holding price up. So, we've left our FLAG with an engulf, before carrying on towards the FL below (we're in a FL PAZ so we want the other side) but before we can carry on we need to go back to the source of our engulf, the RBD that caused the drop. After returning to our source we then engulf the previous low, creating the FL/FTR. Jumping ahead, we engulf the upper limit of the red box, our FL FTR. It's no surprise we've looking for the same simple thing, we've engulfed so price wants to go back to the source of the move, the decision. Why had price hit it's target? When we engulfed the engulf of our red FL we created a new FL. The decision to engulf and create this new FL and enter the PAZ above was the source of the move and was where the orders were as strong upward movement from the source shows. There were other things to indicate we were going to return to this point. Price move in to CP above and then broke the source(FL) of the move in to the CP making our blue FL the target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 6/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Effex, did my answer to the other question answer your as well? I don't wish for you to pass on all of your hard work so easily. Robbie So sorry, forgot to address the above. Think of FLs as the decision to engulf, retrace and move out. It is NOT the original move that caused price to act as it did. Get me? FTRs are certainly demand or supply. They tend to get fake out often.... Why? Because ITs want better fills. And where better than the original FL? Yes sometimes it can go all the way to source. But generally FLs will hold on FTB which then becomes FTR after a HH or LL. Also I always catch nice trades with the QM/FO formed between the FL and subsequent FTR. Look for them and you'll see how often those are A+ setups. The basic premise of the system is that the markets move sharply when they move. If there is a sudden range expansion in the market that has been trading narrowly, human nature is to try an fade that price move. When you get a range expansion, the market is sending you a very loud, clear signal that the market is getting ready to move in the direction of that expansion' Pure gold, does that remind you of anything? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 20/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community RobHbOieM'sE JournEaDlU&CAOTbIOseNrvatioFnOsRUM MORE LOGOUT #121 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Morning mate, so the PAZ article states this; 'So what is a Price Action Zone? A PAZ is exactly what it says; it's a zone on your charts that show a distinct type of Price Action! These distinct zones include poles, flags, stacked supply/demand and compression. Once we are able to accurately determine where these zones are, there is one rule. ONLY trade the edges!' The most important part, when you've identified a PAZ, You wait for an engulf, this is the signal to trade the edge. A nice place to look is an area where there has been a pssible FO, you want to look for the engulf that confirms the FO. On this picture the red box, or PAZ is basically boxing up a swing high and low. After the FO we engulf the decison that took us above the swing high, sellers are now in control, best bet is to wait for a good op to sell, there was a quick scalp straight away but I'm going to cover that later. We then engulf the swing low, and more importantly, the decision holding it up. We now look to the extreme of the PAZ. Our sell point just happens to be a QM level, ever wonder why they're such good places to sell? I would say that they're probably always the edge of a PAZ. I'd go and look for some examples of that and mark everything up, they key thing I think, is to remember this is a market place and decisions are being made on price, so the most important thing to remember is to look to where the decisions were made. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 21/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community This is the GBDUSD M1 from last night. I've only really started progressing in a big way in the last few weeks, FLs were holding me back because of all of the conflicting info. This is my view. For me, the key decision, is one that exerts momentum and breaks a key level, I would call this a FL and I would call the first time back to this (after the subsequent engulf of where the FL took price) an important FTR. If I create the FTR without returning to my important FL, I will expect a FO with momentum towards my FL and most definately away, if price stalled too much at the FL when I'm expecting a FO to it I would thnk twice about the situation. I don't think I would ever use an FTR as a place to trade to be honest, not without considering the FL it is reacting off i.e. the FTR fully consumed the FL. One massive barrier to get over (and then fully understand, which is where I'm at now) is that it's all about scale. Our FL is an imbalance within balance. We're told a FLAG is consolidation, prices are bouncing around in an area where there are no overwhelming buyers of selling to take it beyond the FLAG. Our FL is the first sign of imbalance, so the greater the momentum from our FL the better. A strong imbalance indicates too https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 23/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community many orders which means we have orders left to be filled. Same picture but different scale; 11/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=125 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I hope what I'm saying is clear, we are trading in a market place where decisions are made on the value of something. Now I ask you, is it better to trade a consequence of these decisions or the decisions themselves? Be patient, wait for price to return to a decision. For use what RTM has taught use, let price hit the decision and watch for your confirmation engulf higher or lower, then look for the key decision, the first sign that showed you the reversal of buying or selling strength, the stronger the move from the decision, the stronger the strength, the more are's there are likely to be left there. This is what we want to piggy back on. Remember,. we are not moving the market, we are jumping of the backs of those who are. This is all my own opinion formed through hours, days, weeks and months of reading and proving concepts until my brain couldn't function anymore. Look for the decisions that build price structure. If anyone has an alternative view please tell me, so we can investigate and come to an understanding of what is happening. ( IF ) Posts: 268 Thank you received: 574 Hi Robbie, even not being an expert too, I would like to give all the possible support as well, as I think we are all rowing in the same direction. I may be of course wrong, so we can discuss our opinions. First of all my thought about FLs: they ARE a decision. 26/44 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=150 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 7 - RTM Community But not to Engulf. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT They are a decision that an EG is a real EG and not a FO ! Therefore important. Let's think about a rally: Price EG the last Supply level (SR or Supply) and searches for the next higher Balance (it is all a matter of Lots in both directions). As soon as it finds it, it stops (that's the balance). Then Supply takes over again, using all Buy Orders and looking for below further balance. In case of a FO, there is no Demand there anymore, so it still drops. In case of Real EG the Buy orders take over again at a certain point, and the rally starts again - Taking over all the above Supply. That's the Battle Won ! That's the DP and the FTR and the end (Limit) of the F.L. About not receiving any feedback, I think we cannot expect any Master stopping by in all the posts. But what I am trying to do is to get the only real feedback from the charts. In other words: you have a theory about reading price, look at it in some charts. If you don't get the imagined reaction, then it sounds like some Master said: "you are wrong, look at our Journals again". Perhaps (and here I fully agree with Distinction) we all sometimes want too much. To may see any singular reason of any singular move in price. That's Greedy: one of Trading's Devils. Or we are impatient and want to see all possible setup for not waiting any longer and TRADE ! My suggestion (and my point of this Journey too): try to Master one Set Up only. Forget about PAZ for a while, and look at SR of HTF. I'm going to carry on posting but I might take a little break to gather what I know. I think I've got the idea when it comes to decisions. For instance, in the diagram in the last post, lets assume A came back and consumed all of the orders in B, we would then have a mini compression PAZ. we would be well within our rights to take a trade at A but we would have to have our stop below B. In essence, this is when the FTR in A is at it's strongest, because it has consumed the orders in the FL below, it then becomes the last line of defense, this doesn't mean a reaction is going to take the price in to space, but we should get a reaction at least, to the last decision. We get in to confusing territory when multiple decisions have not been revisited and they are stacked, I believe this to be a PAZ as well but I'm unsure how to approach it. What I mean by this is every decision is reacting to a decision, If the first decision that started the ball rolling hasn't been consumed we have to expect price to get back there. This is very hard to explain, but think of a small decision, this decison decides to engulf something, the FTB to this decision forms a flag, this flag is then a decision to engulf something else, the FTB to this Flag then forms a flag, if the momentum leaving the flag continues in the same direction again and engulfs something else, we have another decision https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 2/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT It's CP if it's consumed the last orders in each decision, I believe it's stacked demand if it hasn't, either way, from what I've seen they both work in a similar way. Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 The flag limit is the feature which defines the flag. As Robbie stated previously, where the flag goes to beyond the limit is not relevant. (Well, it is relevant in so much as where the areas of S/D are within the flag - as this is where price will react on return, once the flag limit is broken. So, the diagram you've posted above does not show the flag limit, it shows where the flag was (maybe) broken. The full story would be something like I've marked here, where the blue box is the flag limit, defined by the points noted in the green circles ( the FL as the abbreviation). So, what's marked as B is the Decision Point (DP) to break the flag and A is the FTR created after that break. What I see is that the FTR was the price point the ITs used to be aiming for in the past, and would FO to the DP (B) but nowadays, they aim for B, and what was once the FO zone, has become the new target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 15/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: antraxia, Paichayon, staurum Roads? Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 Harsh, I don't often like to think about "why" as that can sometimes lead to curve fitting. In the case of A and B, yes, they are both tradeable. A is an FTR, and so I don't think I need explain why or how that is a good place to enter. B is the DP to break the flag limit, and as such is a very important decision. In recent times, this DP has taken on more significance. It used to be the FO area for the FTR, but I beleive that nowaday it has taken on more significance than that. So, to trade around these areas one MUST consider LTF price action, as price may turn at either A or B, and we want to determine which as best we can. It is good practice to look inside A and B on the LTFs for the significant areas of S and D within. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 23/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: harshrthakkar, staurum LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE Roads? Price engulfs the high to the left and returns to the decision to engulf below, notice the nice RBR below? well this forms the rally of the bigger RBR. 2. Price FTRs off the decision below (demand) and shoots away, we now have our FL upon the engulf of the new high. 3. Price exhausts itself at 3 and hits the supply at the FO to the left.. 4. We then move slowly down to 4 where we have our FTB to the FL, notice of the way down we removed all of the supply? 5. 3 and 4 define the limits of our flag, at 5 we engulf the upper limit as well as the FO to the left. 6. we try to get back in to the flag at 6 but hit demand at the decision to leave the FLAG. We CP away from 6 removing all of the buy orders. we eventually break 5 and form our FL/FTR. 7. I say this because the decision must be the origin of the imbalance therefore where the orders are, a failure to return is just a reaction, not a decision. When something is a decision and a failure to return that's a whole different thing and I would say is very important. Where I was going wrong in my definition of FL's was not accepting we can made new FLs as we engulf each FL in a single move. I was placing more importance on the FL that made the most important decision. I now accept this and am happy to stick with it. Something else that I have been working on is that everything moves in threes and that the institutions are lazy. the strength of their moves are repeated, over and over again and this creates symmetry. we can see this through making basic S/R lines.Id we look at the structure of RBR's and DBD's in every time frame we can see that the moves are measured, they don not look the same but from decision to decision the carry a very similar strength. This is going to require a bit more nailing down but you'll see how it ties in with s and d levels to the left, quite convenient. That's my 2 cents for the day. Good trading everyone. I hope that you have since found ways to accept and manage the ambiguity that exists in trading. I found out that ambiguity is addressed with discipline and adherence to an acceptable risk management plan. Define your tolerances and don't accept anything less, this allows you to accept your losses or 'incorrect judgement'. It's really not talked about at much lenth on RTM, but positive expectancy cannot be realised without https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 21/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community kneeling down to the almighty topic of risk management. Something else that I help me back was this very simple statement 'only trade the edges', This is the key but it's absolutely useless information unless you know how to define an edge. What I've come to realise is that unless you have a definitive place to put your SL and TP then you probably shouldn't take the trade. There are exceptions but i believe they come with experience. PAZ's are supposed to be the last piece of the puzzle, for me they are the most important piece and they have clearly been my pursuit for a long time now. I now draw my over arching PAZ's and then use them to track important orders. Risk management, nothing more important I'd say. You could master one set up and barely understand price movements at all but if you know when your set up works and know where you SL and TP should go you are on your way to being successful as long as you have good money management. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 23/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The reason i'm still here is because I want to help people, I'm no expert, yet, but my knowledge and experience is increasing all of the time. It's a very complex top down, due to the large amount of contradictions that become evident in market analysis, and I will do my best to articulate what I am thinking when I absorb your commentary. I have made a few observations about your charts below. Monthly I largely agree with your views, but am also not attached to the idea, as anything can happen at anytime. That overhead target could take some months (hehe) to get there. Weekly I typed up a rant about fakeouts but I decided to delete it. I urge you and others to use that term very sparingly and focus on what is real and happening in front of you each and every tick. Price follows the path of least resistance. You have outlined the possible resistance areas perfectly well. Your M5 chart has great confluence with your weekly analysis and your H4 analysis, so great job there. As I type I can see that there is definitely some selling pressure here, but how far it will go, is a mystery. How long you hold your short trade will come down to your pain tolerance and how long you're able to wait, but as the market can do anything at anytime, and make new decisions that were not part of an original analysis, it is important to take profit of the table at logical areas on the chart. I am not particularly convinced that price will head to the lower weekly target before the overhead target and visa versa, and I don't think the market has decided on that yet either, hence why we have had weeks and months of ranging. Let's put it this way, the long term charts are useful to put our shorter term trades into some https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 29/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community overall context, they are much much harder to use as long term target practice if you're HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT not the patient type. New decisions have to be made. H4 Yes, sure, it was an engulf at one point in time, but the whole move that caused the engulf, has been engulfed itself. Pay credence to the new information on the chart, which contradicts your original views. There are many engulfs on a chart that are no longer 'useful' engulfs, as a chart evolves. You will have to discover this for yourself over many laborious months of charting M5 I like the idea of a touch trade on the upper blue box for entry and the stop above the lower red line. Maximise that risk reward A strategy, if you get stopped out, is to wait for bearish PA on the M5 chart within the entire M30 zone to signal you back in. (For what it's worth your analysis sent some pips my way, I took the trade.) In summary Because I've crapped on a bit, some things to be mindful of: 1. The word 'fake' often comes with negative connotations and thus 'fakeout' can be harmful, be very careful of the language and terms you use to describe your trading as depending on how you have a term defined in your mind, it will affect how you take action and execute your trading plan. 2. An analysis of a chart in one moment of time will not always be relevant by the time you make the trade. A good analysis requires constant information gathering using multiple time frames and from pre-trade to post-trade. Look for a trade at a flag limit. 2. remember the flag limit is itself a flag so look for the associated FTR. 3. Within the FTR look for the point at which the decision was made to turn price in the opposite direction, which I believe was an FTR in itself. 4. Price returned to the FTR, within the FTR of the FL. A mouth full but makes sense. Firstly I'll add the caveat that I'm no expert and this is just what I believe is happening from what I've learned so far. To be honest, most things have started falling in to place in the last few weeks for me, I started looking at the questions as we already have all of the answers. So first question ' Hi robbie, can you expain more with IF's chart about 'engulf, return to source of move'? and why price 'had hit its target'?' https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 5/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In IF's chart I believe the first blue box is the FLAG, the red box isn't the FL of this FLAG, this is an important point to remember. A FL in the past held that flag up and the break of the FL in the past creates a new FL holding price down. Bearing this in mind, the last blue box is our new FL, holding price up. So, we've left our FLAG with an engulf, before carrying on towards the FL below (we're in a FL PAZ so we want the other side) but before we can carry on we need to go back to the source of our engulf, the RBD that caused the drop. After returning to our source we then engulf the previous low, creating the FL/FTR. Jumping ahead, we engulf the upper limit of the red box, our FL FTR. It's no surprise we've looking for the same simple thing, we've engulfed so price wants to go back to the source of the move, the decision. Why had price hit it's target? When we engulfed the engulf of our red FL we created a new FL. The decision to engulf and create this new FL and enter the PAZ above was the source of the move and was where the orders were as strong upward movement from the source shows. There were other things to indicate we were going to return to this point. Price move in to CP above and then broke the source(FL) of the move in to the CP making our blue FL the target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 6/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Effex, did my answer to the other question answer your as well? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE 11/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community THANK YOU EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME Robbie's Journal & Observations #36 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Hi all, I'm really starting to question the way flags are drawn now. It seems fine to determine one limit of the flag, for instance, if price is moving higher, breaks some SR supply and creatres an FTR/DBD we have one limit but what determines the next limit, is it the flag limit above? this doesn't work out, the only explanation can be that when prices returns to the FL/FTR which is created after breaking something significant, the previous high or low becomes the other flag boundary. It seems misleading saying that the flag limit above and below are the boundaries to the flag. the second limit can only be created after price has returned to the first limit? Does that make sense? So, this is why price returns to the base of a pole when the first limit of the flag is broken. I'll investigate more tomorrow. Night all. Plan your trade & trade your plan. It seems fine to determine one limit of the flag, for instance, if price is moving higher, breaks some SR supply and creatres an FTR/DBD we have one limit but what determines the next limit, is it the flag limit above? this doesn't work out, the only explanation can be that when prices returns to the FL/FTR which is created after breaking something significant, the previous high or low becomes the other flag boundary. It seems misleading saying that the flag limit above and below are the boundaries to the flag. the second limit can only be created after price has returned to the first limit? Does that make sense? So, this is why price returns to the base of a pole when the first limit of the flag is broken. I'll investigate more tomorrow. Night all. Or take profits and enter on the retrace targeting the edge of the next zone. practice defining zones and correctly defining the origin. Research the FTR of a FO, price seems to like to FO to this area. I don't wish for you to pass on all of your hard work so easily. Robbie So sorry, forgot to address the above. Think of FLs as the decision to engulf, retrace and move out. It is NOT the original move that caused price to act as it did. Get me? FTRs are certainly demand or supply. They tend to get fake out often.... Why? Because ITs want better fills. And where better than the original FL? Yes sometimes it can go all the way to source. But generally FLs will hold on FTB which then becomes FTR after a HH or LL. Also I always catch nice trades with the QM/FO formed between the FL and subsequent FTR. Look for them and you'll see how often those are A+ setups. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 20/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community RobHbOieM'sE JournEaDlU&CAOTbIOseNrvatioFnOsRUM MORE LOGOUT #121 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Morning mate, so the PAZ article states this; 'So what is a Price Action Zone? A PAZ is exactly what it says; it's a zone on your charts that show a distinct type of Price Action! These distinct zones include poles, flags, stacked supply/demand and compression. Once we are able to accurately determine where these zones are, there is one rule. ONLY trade the edges!' The most important part, when you've identified a PAZ, You wait for an engulf, this is the signal to trade the edge. A nice place to look is an area where there has been a pssible FO, you want to look for the engulf that confirms the FO. On this picture the red box, or PAZ is basically boxing up a swing high and low. After the FO we engulf the decison that took us above the swing high, sellers are now in control, best bet is to wait for a good op to sell, there was a quick scalp straight away but I'm going to cover that later. We then engulf the swing low, and more importantly, the decision holding it up. We now look to the extreme of the PAZ. Our sell point just happens to be a QM level, ever wonder why they're such good places to sell? I would say that they're probably always the edge of a PAZ. I'd go and look for some examples of that and mark everything up, they key thing I think, is to remember this is a market place and decisions are being made on price, so the most important thing to remember is to look to where the decisions were made. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 21/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community This is the GBDUSD M1 from last night. Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: pucci, staurum 22/31 Plan your trade & trade your plan. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE FORUM MORE LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #122 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Morning Antraxia, Thank you very much! I've only really started progressing in a big way in the last few weeks, FLs were holding me back because of all of the conflicting info. This is my view. For me, the key decision, is one that exerts momentum and breaks a key level, I would call this a FL and I would call the first time back to this (after the subsequent engulf of where the FL took price) an important FTR. If I create the FTR without returning to my important FL, I will expect a FO with momentum towards my FL and most definately away, if price stalled too much at the FL when I'm expecting a FO to it I would thnk twice about the situation. I don't think I would ever use an FTR as a place to trade to be honest, not without considering the FL it is reacting off i.e. the FTR fully consumed the FL. One massive barrier to get over (and then fully understand, which is where I'm at now) is that it's all about scale. Our FL is an imbalance within balance. We're told a FLAG is consolidation, prices are bouncing around in an area where there are no overwhelming buyers of selling to take it beyond the FLAG. Our FL is the first sign of imbalance, so the greater the momentum from our FL the better. A strong imbalance indicates too https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 23/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community many orders which means we have orders left to be filled. Same picture but different scale; 11/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=125 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I hope what I'm saying is clear, we are trading in a market place where decisions are made on the value of something. Now I ask you, is it better to trade a consequence of these decisions or the decisions themselves? Be patient, wait for price to return to a decision. For use what RTM has taught use, let price hit the decision and watch for your confirmation engulf higher or lower, then look for the key decision, the first sign that showed you the reversal of buying or selling strength, the stronger the move from the decision, the stronger the strength, the more are's there are likely to be left there. This is what we want to piggy back on. Remember,. we are not moving the market, we are jumping of the backs of those who are. This is all my own opinion formed through hours, days, weeks and months of reading and proving concepts until my brain couldn't function anymore. Look for the decisions that build price structure. If anyone has an alternative view please tell me, so we can investigate and come to an understanding of what is happening. I'll mention FTR's but I don't really care about them in a way to trade from unless they are the base in a RBR or DBD that decides to engulf another decision of the same magnitude. ( IF ) Posts: 268 Thank you received: 574 Hi Robbie, even not being an expert too, I would like to give all the possible support as well, as I think we are all rowing in the same direction. First of all my thought about FLs: they ARE a decision. 26/44 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=150 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 7 - RTM Community But not to Engulf. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT They are a decision that an EG is a real EG and not a FO ! Therefore important. Let's think about a rally: Price EG the last Supply level (SR or Supply) and searches for the next higher Balance (it is all a matter of Lots in both directions). As soon as it finds it, it stops (that's the balance). Then Supply takes over again, using all Buy Orders and looking for below further balance. In case of a FO, there is no Demand there anymore, so it still drops. In case of Real EG the Buy orders take over again at a certain point, and the rally starts again - Taking over all the above Supply. That's the Battle Won ! That's the DP and the FTR and the end (Limit) of the F.L. About not receiving any feedback, I think we cannot expect any Master stopping by in all the posts. But what I am trying to do is to get the only real feedback from the charts. In other words: you have a theory about reading price, look at it in some charts. If you don't get the imagined reaction, then it sounds like some Master said: "you are wrong, look at our Journals again". Perhaps (and here I fully agree with Distinction) we all sometimes want too much. To may see any singular reason of any singular move in price. That's Greedy: one of Trading's Devils. Or we are impatient and want to see all possible setup for not waiting any longer and TRADE ! My suggestion (and my point of this Journey too): try to Master one Set Up only. Forget about PAZ for a while, and look at SR of HTF. EG => FL => FTR => FTB. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=150 27/44 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I am of the opinion too, that mastering those we could make enough to have no need of further investigating. But till then I would suggest: clear your mind from TOO MUCH (I say to myself as first of course) and learn to WAIT. Psichology will be trained, Focus will be trained and with high probability Balance will grow Sorry for the long post and do not hesitate to critic or reply: mine is not the absolute Truth Just some thoughts after some experience. I'm going to carry on posting but I might take a little break to gather what I know. I think I've got the idea when it comes to decisions. For instance, in the diagram in the last post, lets assume A came back and consumed all of the orders in B, we would then have a mini compression PAZ. we would be well within our rights to take a trade at A but we would have to have our stop below B. In essence, this is when the FTR in A is at it's strongest, because it has consumed the orders in the FL below, it then becomes the last line of defense, this doesn't mean a reaction is going to take the price in to space, but we should get a reaction at least, to the last decision. We get in to confusing territory when multiple decisions have not been revisited and they are stacked, I believe this to be a PAZ as well but I'm unsure how to approach it. What I mean by this is every decision is reacting to a decision, If the first decision that started the ball rolling hasn't been consumed we have to expect price to get back there. This is very hard to explain, but think of a small decision, this decison decides to engulf something, the FTB to this decision forms a flag, this flag is then a decision to engulf something else, the FTB to this Flag then forms a flag, if the momentum leaving the flag continues in the same direction again and engulfs something else, we have another decision https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 2/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT It's CP if it's consumed the last orders in each decision, I believe it's stacked demand if it hasn't, either way, from what I've seen they both work in a similar way. Hope that makes sense, Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: staurum Plan your trade & trade your plan. Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 The flag limit is the feature which defines the flag. As Robbie stated previously, where the flag goes to beyond the limit is not relevant. (Well, it is relevant in so much as where the areas of S/D are within the flag - as this is where price will react on return, once the flag limit is broken. So, the diagram you've posted above does not show the flag limit, it shows where the flag was (maybe) broken. The full story would be something like I've marked here, where the blue box is the flag limit, defined by the points noted in the green circles ( the FL as the abbreviation). So, what's marked as B is the Decision Point (DP) to break the flag and A is the FTR created after that break. What I see is that the FTR was the price point the ITs used to be aiming for in the past, and would FO to the DP (B) but nowadays, they aim for B, and what was once the FO zone, has become the new target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 15/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: antraxia, Paichayon, staurum Roads? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #188 harshrthakkar Offline Trader https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 22/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community Posts: 569 Thank you received: 551 HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Referring # 182 of RCMAF, A question here,are bith A and B tradable and which one is preferred,why? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #189 rcmacf Offline Associate Trader What...Me? Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 Harsh, I don't often like to think about "why" as that can sometimes lead to curve fitting. In the case of A and B, yes, they are both tradeable. A is an FTR, and so I don't think I need explain why or how that is a good place to enter. B is the DP to break the flag limit, and as such is a very important decision. In recent times, this DP has taken on more significance. It used to be the FO area for the FTR, but I beleive that nowaday it has taken on more significance than that. So, to trade around these areas one MUST consider LTF price action, as price may turn at either A or B, and we want to determine which as best we can. It is good practice to look inside A and B on the LTFs for the significant areas of S and D within. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 23/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: harshrthakkar, staurum LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE Roads? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #202 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Hello V, I'll give this my best shot. I'm still not 100% on IF's reasoning but I'll give it ago. 1. Price engulfs the high to the left and returns to the decision to engulf below, notice the nice RBR below? well this forms the rally of the bigger RBR. 2. Price FTRs off the decision below (demand) and shoots away, we now have our FL upon the engulf of the new high. 3. Price exhausts itself at 3 and hits the supply at the FO to the left.. 4. We then move slowly down to 4 where we have our FTB to the FL, notice of the way down we removed all of the supply? 5. 3 and 4 define the limits of our flag, at 5 we engulf the upper limit as well as the FO to the left. 6. we try to get back in to the flag at 6 but hit demand at the decision to leave the FLAG. We CP away from 6 removing all of the buy orders. we eventually break 5 and form our FL/FTR. 7. Price then rallies up to 7 where we hit some S/R from the past. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 3/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community 8. At 8 we have our FTB to our FL and we remove the last of the supply. This area HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT shouldn't cause to much of an issue in the future. I believe 8 then becomes the limit of our CPPAZ. 9. at 9 we engulf 7. 10, we fail to return at 10. 11. At 11 we engulf the FL low at 10. 12, we rally and try to get back in to the flag above at 12 then we get rejected by supply at the cap on price. 13. We then start making lower lows and lower highs towards the base of the blue flag we have just entered. 12 is now an important FTR. we are aiming for the supply at the edge of the CPPAZ at 8 where we should get a small reaction at least. 14. we retrace back to the supply above at 14 which is a nice cap on price. 15 At 15 we engulf in to the CPPAZ, and want the other side, we retrace back to the last supply at 14, the origin of the move/decision to engulf. we drop through the CPPAZ and through the other side to the base of the pole at 4 where the sells overwhelmed the little demand that was left it that area. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT 16. So at 16 we engulf the lower limit of the blue flag and also the FL holding it up. 17. price retraces to 17 where there is a strong s/r flip right on the edge of the FTR which was created after engulfing in to the CPPAZ. I don't know why IF put the red box around the swing high? Maybe because it was stacked supply? From 17 we can take a trade knowing that price wants to go down to the FL at 18 because of the engulf in to the FLAG. So, to answer your question, i think IF means we should follow the key engulfs of these flags especially when they are forming either side of key s/r levels. as we cross one of these levels and FTR we are flipping sentiment on the value, what was once a great place to buy, is now a great place to sell. OK so, this is where things aren't straight forward, so how do we define a FLAG? How do we know the limits? after leaving a flag limit, lets say it's a demand FL, how do we know where the high is? is it the point reached before we come back to the demand of the FL, I say this because often we will only hit the s/r of the FL, engulf the high and then come back down to the body/demand of the FL. so would the high of the FLAG in that scenario be define by the high reached upon leaving the FL or the HIGH reached before returning to the body/demand of the FL? I think this is very important for us to nail down, If there is a flag then there is a way of defining it. IF, can you clear that up for us? To simplify, is the limit the high reached after leaving the FL, i.e. the high of the rally in the RBR? or is it the high reached before the FTB to the demand of the FL? I.E. not the s/r of the FL? HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT So far it is quite straight forward creating the plan, the hard part is WHAT I'm going to trade and defining the specific conditions I require. For me, I need this proven concept, something that i can rinse and repeat, day in, day out. I'm finding it hard to define the concept that suits me, I like the idea of decisions being made rather than price just simply failing to return, the decision to engulf (which is made before an engulf) is logically more important than price failing to return after an engulf. I say this because the decision must be the origin of the imbalance therefore where the orders are, a failure to return is just a reaction, not a decision. When something is a decision and a failure to return that's a whole different thing and I would say is very important. Where I was going wrong in my definition of FL's was not accepting we can made new FLs as we engulf each FL in a single move. I was placing more importance on the FL that made the most important decision. I now accept this and am happy to stick with it. Something else that I have been working on is that everything moves in threes and that the institutions are lazy. the strength of their moves are repeated, over and over again and this creates symmetry. we can see this through making basic S/R lines.Id we look at the structure of RBR's and DBD's in every time frame we can see that the moves are measured, they don not look the same but from decision to decision the carry a very similar strength. This is going to require a bit more nailing down but you'll see how it ties in with s and d levels to the left, quite convenient. That's my 2 cents for the day. Good trading everyone. QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Hello Robbie #211 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 20/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT ShadowPit Offline Associate Trader Posts: 277 Thank you received: 1195 Hi Robbie, Popping by to say hello. Bit of a message to you and others around here as I can observe that RTM does not have the same solid support hierarchy that once upon a time it thrived in. It has taken me the better part of today to read your journal from beginning to end. Great job from the get go, you had me sucked in with your maturing narrative and application of the technical skills you have learned. You ask all the right questions which I can see is driven by your desire to have all the definitive answers. Your lengthy pursuit of discovering a fixed and collectively accepted definition of a FL resonated well with me, and I came to realise it was something I never fully reconciled for myself as I was absorbing the teachings here. There comes a point in time where ones thirst for finite answers exceeds the information accessable and the support provided, but this is where one must rise above their frustrations and move on with cautious optimism on the back of the valuable knowledge they have gained and their own unique mindset, and I am not just talking about trading here. I can appreciate your comments that the support network didn't even meet your minimum expectations, and can empathise how frustrating that must have felt. It's good to see you are still around because I feel that there is a lot traders can learn from your style of learning, observing, journaling and path of discovery, that seem to be absent in others' threads. I hope that you have since found ways to accept and manage the ambiguity that exists in trading. I found out that ambiguity is addressed with discipline and adherence to an acceptable risk management plan. Define your tolerances and don't accept anything less, this allows you to accept your losses or 'incorrect judgement'. It's really not talked about at much lenth on RTM, but positive expectancy cannot be realised without https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 21/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community kneeling down to the almighty topic of risk management. I think you are already at a HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT point in your trading where you accept you don't have all the answers but can succeed regardless. It's all about learning by doing now, you have circled the theory from every which way and the next level of understanding comes with immersing yourself in the process and not second guessing your decisions in real time. Unfortunately I had not been around at the right time as part of the support network here, and it was a little hard to read your frustrations go unaddressed as I digested your thread. Don't give up on your pursuit. p.s. Would love to see more of your analysis in the context of the HTF price structures! I had a read through my journal not to long ago and i could see my ups and downs and how I would go back and forward on what I was thinking. You know, when I first started https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 22/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community looking at these methods I would see things and disregard them and no one had HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT mentioned it, I should have trusted myself. I had many questions, most still unanswered, but maybe because to answer them would have been to give too much away? I don't know, but I've eventually came to my own conclusions that make sense and fit with the methods. The FL was a big bone of contention for me and still is to be honest, maybe the article was valid once upon a time but i believe it is truly out of date now, only because there is a little more to it that we need to be aware of, but again, maybe that's the point. My pursuit for all of the answers was brutal and was actually quite hard mentally, I realised that this train of thought would actually be my downfall when I come to trade, i needed to realign my expectations and what I wanted from the market, and more importantly how I could achieve it. I would have loved some constructive direction, especially at the start of the year when I was near enough the only person posting but it never came, with what I know now I believe I could have been helped without someone giving away the answers. I eventually just looked to the charts to figure out each turn in price, it wasn't good enough for me for something like the QM or CP to just been something to look for and work, I had to know why. I believe people starting to learn now should keep this in the back of their minds at all times. Something else that I help me back was this very simple statement 'only trade the edges', This is the key but it's absolutely useless information unless you know how to define an edge. What I've come to realise is that unless you have a definitive place to put your SL and TP then you probably shouldn't take the trade. There are exceptions but i believe they come with experience. PAZ's are supposed to be the last piece of the puzzle, for me they are the most important piece and they have clearly been my pursuit for a long time now. I now draw my over arching PAZ's and then use them to track important orders. Risk management, nothing more important I'd say. You could master one set up and barely understand price movements at all but if you know when your set up works and know where you SL and TP should go you are on your way to being successful as long as you have good money management. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 23/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT The reason i'm still here is because I want to help people, I'm no expert, yet, but my knowledge and experience is increasing all of the time. I quit my job a few months ago to spend more time with my terminally ill Dad, trading will have to be my income soon. I will carry on posting and helping people for as long as I can. Discipline, patience and a plan, there is so much more to trading than the charts. We will defeat ourselves of we don't accept our limitations, at least to a degree. I really appreciate you taking the time to post. I'll put up some HTF analysis later, on USDCHF, I believe we have a big short OP very very close. It's a very complex top down, due to the large amount of contradictions that become evident in market analysis, and I will do my best to articulate what I am thinking when I absorb your commentary. I have made a few observations about your charts below. Monthly I largely agree with your views, but am also not attached to the idea, as anything can happen at anytime. That overhead target could take some months (hehe) to get there. Weekly I typed up a rant about fakeouts but I decided to delete it. I urge you and others to use that term very sparingly and focus on what is real and happening in front of you each and every tick. Price follows the path of least resistance. You have outlined the possible resistance areas perfectly well. Your M5 chart has great confluence with your weekly analysis and your H4 analysis, so great job there. As I type I can see that there is definitely some selling pressure here, but how far it will go, is a mystery. How long you hold your short trade will come down to your pain tolerance and how long you're able to wait, but as the market can do anything at anytime, and make new decisions that were not part of an original analysis, it is important to take profit of the table at logical areas on the chart. I am not particularly convinced that price will head to the lower weekly target before the overhead target and visa versa, and I don't think the market has decided on that yet either, hence why we have had weeks and months of ranging. Let's put it this way, the long term charts are useful to put our shorter term trades into some https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 29/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community overall context, they are much much harder to use as long term target practice if you're HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT not the patient type. New decisions have to be made. H4 Yes, sure, it was an engulf at one point in time, but the whole move that caused the engulf, has been engulfed itself. Pay credence to the new information on the chart, which contradicts your original views. There are many engulfs on a chart that are no longer 'useful' engulfs, as a chart evolves. You will have to discover this for yourself over many laborious months of charting M5 I like the idea of a touch trade on the upper blue box for entry and the stop above the lower red line. Maximise that risk reward A strategy, if you get stopped out, is to wait for bearish PA on the M5 chart within the entire M30 zone to signal you back in. (For what it's worth your analysis sent some pips my way, I took the trade.) In summary Because I've crapped on a bit, some things to be mindful of: 1. The word 'fake' often comes with negative connotations and thus 'fakeout' can be harmful, be very careful of the language and terms you use to describe your trading as depending on how you have a term defined in your mind, it will affect how you take action and execute your trading plan. 2. An analysis of a chart in one moment of time will not always be relevant by the time you make the trade. A good analysis requires constant information gathering using multiple time frames and from pre-trade to post-trade. I set myself the task this afternoon to look for a trade in the past that I may have taken using the knowledge from RTM. On top of that I wanted to find the real reason price turned at that exact point. Hopefully the charts below will show you what I have found. My thought process was this; 1. Look for a trade at a flag limit. 2. remember the flag limit is itself a flag so look for the associated FTR. 3. Within the FTR look for the point at which the decision was made to turn price in the opposite direction, which I believe was an FTR in itself. 4. Price returned to the FTR, within the FTR of the FL. A mouth full but makes sense. I'm going to find more of these examples but here is the first; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations 18/40 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Any feedback is much appreciated. How many trades will I have open at any one time? 4. How many pairs/commodities will I monitor? I can't monitor them all, which ones will I chose? etc What I've been looking for are basic rules, rules to follow the market, rules to put on top of my foundation of money management. Firstly I'll add the caveat that I'm no expert and this is just what I believe is happening from what I've learned so far. To be honest, most things have started falling in to place in the last few weeks for me, I started looking at the questions as we already have all of the answers. So first question ' Hi robbie, can you expain more with IF's chart about 'engulf, return to source of move'? and why price 'had hit its target'?' https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 5/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT In IF's chart I believe the first blue box is the FLAG, the red box isn't the FL of this FLAG, this is an important point to remember. A FL in the past held that flag up and the break of the FL in the past creates a new FL holding price down. Bearing this in mind, the last blue box is our new FL, holding price up. So, we've left our FLAG with an engulf, before carrying on towards the FL below (we're in a FL PAZ so we want the other side) but before we can carry on we need to go back to the source of our engulf, the RBD that caused the drop. After returning to our source we then engulf the previous low, creating the FL/FTR. Jumping ahead, we engulf the upper limit of the red box, our FL FTR. It's no surprise we've looking for the same simple thing, we've engulfed so price wants to go back to the source of the move, the decision. Why had price hit it's target? When we engulfed the engulf of our red FL we created a new FL. The decision to engulf and create this new FL and enter the PAZ above was the source of the move and was where the orders were as strong upward movement from the source shows. There were other things to indicate we were going to return to this point. Price move in to CP above and then broke the source(FL) of the move in to the CP making our blue FL the target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=25 6/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 2 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Effex, did my answer to the other question answer your as well? It seems fine to determine one limit of the flag, for instance, if price is moving higher, breaks some SR supply and creatres an FTR/DBD we have one limit but what determines the next limit, is it the flag limit above? this doesn't work out, the only explanation can be that when prices returns to the FL/FTR which is created after breaking something significant, the previous high or low becomes the other flag boundary. I believe they are, I believe they're trying to give us false readings, fooling us in to thinking our FLs are higher/lower than we think. And where better than the original FL? Yes sometimes it can go all the way to source. But generally FLs will hold on FTB which then becomes FTR after a HH or LL. Also I always catch nice trades with the QM/FO formed between the FL and subsequent FTR. Look for them and you'll see how often those are A+ setups. The basic premise of the system is that the markets move sharply when they move. If there is a sudden range expansion in the market that has been trading narrowly, human nature is to try an fade that price move. When you get a range expansion, the market is sending you a very loud, clear signal that the market is getting ready to move in the direction of that expansion' Pure gold, does that remind you of anything? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 20/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community RobHbOieM'sE JournEaDlU&CAOTbIOseNrvatioFnOsRUM MORE LOGOUT #121 Rdk123 Offline Trader Posts: 282 Thank you received: 288 Morning mate, so the PAZ article states this; 'So what is a Price Action Zone? A PAZ is exactly what it says; it's a zone on your charts that show a distinct type of Price Action! These distinct zones include poles, flags, stacked supply/demand and compression. Once we are able to accurately determine where these zones are, there is one rule. ONLY trade the edges!' The most important part, when you've identified a PAZ, You wait for an engulf, this is the signal to trade the edge. A nice place to look is an area where there has been a pssible FO, you want to look for the engulf that confirms the FO. On this picture the red box, or PAZ is basically boxing up a swing high and low. After the FO we engulf the decison that took us above the swing high, sellers are now in control, best bet is to wait for a good op to sell, there was a quick scalp straight away but I'm going to cover that later. We then engulf the swing low, and more importantly, the decision holding it up. We now look to the extreme of the PAZ. Our sell point just happens to be a QM level, ever wonder why they're such good places to sell? I would say that they're probably always the edge of a PAZ. I'd go and look for some examples of that and mark everything up, they key thing I think, is to remember this is a market place and decisions are being made on price, so the most important thing to remember is to look to where the decisions were made. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 21/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community This is the GBDUSD M1 from last night. I've only really started progressing in a big way in the last few weeks, FLs were holding me back because of all of the conflicting info. This is my view. For me, the key decision, is one that exerts momentum and breaks a key level, I would call this a FL and I would call the first time back to this (after the subsequent engulf of where the FL took price) an important FTR. If I create the FTR without returning to my important FL, I will expect a FO with momentum towards my FL and most definately away, if price stalled too much at the FL when I'm expecting a FO to it I would thnk twice about the situation. I don't think I would ever use an FTR as a place to trade to be honest, not without considering the FL it is reacting off i.e. the FTR fully consumed the FL. One massive barrier to get over (and then fully understand, which is where I'm at now) is that it's all about scale. Our FL is an imbalance within balance. We're told a FLAG is consolidation, prices are bouncing around in an area where there are no overwhelming buyers of selling to take it beyond the FLAG. Our FL is the first sign of imbalance, so the greater the momentum from our FL the better. A strong imbalance indicates too https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=100 23/31 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 5 - RTM Community many orders which means we have orders left to be filled. Problem number one; https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=125 10/35 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Actually, this is my only problem, as if we can correctly identify the decision to engulf (DBD/RBR) or as markepedia calls it, a FL then we can track price through time as long as we apply the correct scale. Here is an example which includes a trade to show we can take what we want from the market as long as we are aware of it's limitations. Same picture but different scale; 11/35 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=125 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 6 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I hope what I'm saying is clear, we are trading in a market place where decisions are made on the value of something. Now I ask you, is it better to trade a consequence of these decisions or the decisions themselves? Be patient, wait for price to return to a decision. For use what RTM has taught use, let price hit the decision and watch for your confirmation engulf higher or lower, then look for the key decision, the first sign that showed you the reversal of buying or selling strength, the stronger the move from the decision, the stronger the strength, the more are's there are likely to be left there. This is what we want to piggy back on. Remember,. we are not moving the market, we are jumping of the backs of those who are. This is all my own opinion formed through hours, days, weeks and months of reading and proving concepts until my brain couldn't function anymore. Look for the decisions that build price structure. If anyone has an alternative view please tell me, so we can investigate and come to an understanding of what is happening. ( IF ) Posts: 268 Thank you received: 574 Hi Robbie, even not being an expert too, I would like to give all the possible support as well, as I think we are all rowing in the same direction. I may be of course wrong, so we can discuss our opinions. First of all my thought about FLs: they ARE a decision. 26/44 https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=150 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 7 - RTM Community But not to Engulf. HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT They are a decision that an EG is a real EG and not a FO ! Therefore important. Let's think about a rally: Price EG the last Supply level (SR or Supply) and searches for the next higher Balance (it is all a matter of Lots in both directions). As soon as it finds it, it stops (that's the balance). Then Supply takes over again, using all Buy Orders and looking for below further balance. In case of a FO, there is no Demand there anymore, so it still drops. In case of Real EG the Buy orders take over again at a certain point, and the rally starts again - Taking over all the above Supply. That's the Battle Won ! That's the DP and the FTR and the end (Limit) of the F.L. About not receiving any feedback, I think we cannot expect any Master stopping by in all the posts. But what I am trying to do is to get the only real feedback from the charts. In other words: you have a theory about reading price, look at it in some charts. If you don't get the imagined reaction, then it sounds like some Master said: "you are wrong, look at our Journals again". Perhaps (and here I fully agree with Distinction) we all sometimes want too much. To may see any singular reason of any singular move in price. That's Greedy: one of Trading's Devils. Or we are impatient and want to see all possible setup for not waiting any longer and TRADE ! My suggestion (and my point of this Journey too): try to Master one Set Up only. Forget about PAZ for a while, and look at SR of HTF. EG => FL => FTR => FTB. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=150 27/44 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 7 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT I am of the opinion too, that mastering those we could make enough to have no need of further investigating. May be we will want. Then we will study why and when they fail, and price goes further to look for the DP to EG (the origin of Supply). But till then I would suggest: clear your mind from TOO MUCH (I say to myself as first of course) and learn to WAIT. Psichology will be trained, Focus will be trained and with high probability Balance will grow Sorry for the long post and do not hesitate to critic or reply: mine is not the absolute Truth Just some thoughts after some experience. I'm going to carry on posting but I might take a little break to gather what I know. I think I've got the idea when it comes to decisions. For instance, in the diagram in the last post, lets assume A came back and consumed all of the orders in B, we would then have a mini compression PAZ. we would be well within our rights to take a trade at A but we would have to have our stop below B. In essence, this is when the FTR in A is at it's strongest, because it has consumed the orders in the FL below, it then becomes the last line of defense, this doesn't mean a reaction is going to take the price in to space, but we should get a reaction at least, to the last decision. We get in to confusing territory when multiple decisions have not been revisited and they are stacked, I believe this to be a PAZ as well but I'm unsure how to approach it. What I mean by this is every decision is reacting to a decision, If the first decision that started the ball rolling hasn't been consumed we have to expect price to get back there. This is very hard to explain, but think of a small decision, this decison decides to engulf something, the FTB to this decision forms a flag, this flag is then a decision to engulf something else, the FTB to this Flag then forms a flag, if the momentum leaving the flag continues in the same direction again and engulfs something else, we have another decision https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 2/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT It's CP if it's consumed the last orders in each decision, I believe it's stacked demand if it hasn't, either way, from what I've seen they both work in a similar way. Hope that makes sense, Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: staurum Plan your trade & trade your plan. Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 The flag limit is the feature which defines the flag. As Robbie stated previously, where the flag goes to beyond the limit is not relevant. (Well, it is relevant in so much as where the areas of S/D are within the flag - as this is where price will react on return, once the flag limit is broken. So, the diagram you've posted above does not show the flag limit, it shows where the flag was (maybe) broken. The full story would be something like I've marked here, where the blue box is the flag limit, defined by the points noted in the green circles ( the FL as the abbreviation). So, what's marked as B is the Decision Point (DP) to break the flag and A is the FTR created after that break. What I see is that the FTR was the price point the ITs used to be aiming for in the past, and would FO to the DP (B) but nowadays, they aim for B, and what was once the FO zone, has become the new target. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 15/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT Attachments: The following user(s) said Thank You: antraxia, Paichayon, staurum Roads? QUICK REPLY REPLY QUOTE THANK YOU Robbie's Journal & Observations #189 rcmacf Offline Associate Trader What...Me? Posts: 463 Thank you received: 910 Harsh, I don't often like to think about "why" as that can sometimes lead to curve fitting. In the case of A and B, yes, they are both tradeable. A is an FTR, and so I don't think I need explain why or how that is a good place to enter. B is the DP to break the flag limit, and as such is a very important decision. In recent times, this DP has taken on more significance. It used to be the FO area for the FTR, but I beleive that nowaday it has taken on more significance than that. So, to trade around these areas one MUST consider LTF price action, as price may turn at either A or B, and we want to determine which as best we can. It is good practice to look inside A and B on the LTFs for the significant areas of S and D within. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=175 23/39 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 8 - RTM Community The following user(s) said Thank You: harshrthakkar, staurum LOGOUT HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE Roads?
