I'm still not 100% on IF's reasoning but I'll give it ago. 1. Price engulfs the high to the left and returns to the decision to engulf below, notice the nice RBR below? well this forms the rally of the bigger RBR. 2. Price FTRs off the decision below (demand) and shoots away, we now have our FL upon the engulf of the new high. 3. Price exhausts itself at 3 and hits the supply at the FO to the left.. 4. We then move slowly down to 4 where we have our FTB to the FL, notice of the way down we removed all of the supply? 5. 3 and 4 define the limits of our flag, at 5 we engulf the upper limit as well as the FO to the left. 6. we try to get back in to the flag at 6 but hit demand at the decision to leave the FLAG. We CP away from 6 removing all of the buy orders. we eventually break 5 and form our FL/FTR. 7. Price then rallies up to 7 where we hit some S/R from the past. https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 3/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community 8. At 8 we have our FTB to our FL and we remove the last of the supply. This area HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT shouldn't cause to much of an issue in the future. I believe 8 then becomes the limit of our CPPAZ. 9. at 9 we engulf 7. 10, we fail to return at 10. 11. At 11 we engulf the FL low at 10. 12, we rally and try to get back in to the flag above at 12 then we get rejected by supply at the cap on price. 13. We then start making lower lows and lower highs towards the base of the blue flag we have just entered. 12 is now an important FTR. we are aiming for the supply at the edge of the CPPAZ at 8 where we should get a small reaction at least. 14. we retrace back to the supply above at 14 which is a nice cap on price. 15 At 15 we engulf in to the CPPAZ, and want the other side, we retrace back to the last supply at 14, the origin of the move/decision to engulf. we drop through the CPPAZ and through the other side to the base of the pole at 4 where the sells overwhelmed the little demand that was left it that area. So at 16 we engulf the lower limit of the blue flag and also the FL holding it up. 17. price retraces to 17 where there is a strong s/r flip right on the edge of the FTR which was created after engulfing in to the CPPAZ. So, to answer your question, i think IF means we should follow the key engulfs of these flags especially when they are forming either side of key s/r levels. as we cross one of these levels and FTR we are flipping sentiment on the value, what was once a great place to buy, is now a great place to sell. OK so, this is where things aren't straight forward, so how do we define a FLAG? How do we know the limits? after leaving a flag limit, lets say it's a demand FL, how do we know where the high is? is it the point reached before we come back to the demand of the FL, I say this because often we will only hit the s/r of the FL, engulf the high and then come back down to the body/demand of the FL. so would the high of the FLAG in that scenario be define by the high reached upon leaving the FL or the HIGH reached before returning to the body/demand of the FL? I think this is very important for us to nail down, If there is a flag then there is a way of defining it. IF, can you clear that up for us? To simplify, is the limit the high reached after leaving the FL, i.e. the high of the rally in the RBR? or is it the high reached before the FTB to the demand of the FL? I.E. not the s/r of the FL? HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT So far it is quite straight forward creating the plan, the hard part is WHAT I'm going to trade and defining the specific conditions I require. For me, I need this proven concept, something that i can rinse and repeat, day in, day out. I'm finding it hard to define the concept that suits me, I like the idea of decisions being made rather than price just simply failing to return, the decision to engulf (which is made before an engulf) is logically more important than price failing to return after an engulf. I say this because the decision must be the origin of the imbalance therefore where the orders are, a failure to return is just a reaction, not a decision. When something is a decision and a failure to return that's a whole different thing and I would say is very important. Where I was going wrong in my definition of FL's was not accepting we can made new FLs as we engulf each FL in a single move. I was placing more importance on the FL that made the most important decision. I now accept this and am happy to stick with it. Something else that I have been working on is that everything moves in threes and that the institutions are lazy. the strength of their moves are repeated, over and over again and this creates symmetry. we can see this through making basic S/R lines.Id we look at the structure of RBR's and DBD's in every time frame we can see that the moves are measured, they don not look the same but from decision to decision the carry a very similar strength. This is going to require a bit more nailing down but you'll see how it ties in with s and d levels to the left, quite convenient. That's my 2 cents for the day. Good trading everyone. Define your tolerances and don't accept anything less, this allows you to accept your losses or 'incorrect judgement'. It's really not talked about at much lenth on RTM, but positive expectancy cannot be realised without https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 21/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community kneeling down to the almighty topic of risk management. I believe people starting to learn now should keep this in the back of their minds at all times. Something else that I help me back was this very simple statement 'only trade the edges', This is the key but it's absolutely useless information unless you know how to define an edge. What I've come to realise is that unless you have a definitive place to put your SL and TP then you probably shouldn't take the trade. There are exceptions but i believe they come with experience. PAZ's are supposed to be the last piece of the puzzle, for me they are the most important piece and they have clearly been my pursuit for a long time now. I now draw my over arching PAZ's and then use them to track important orders. Risk management, nothing more important I'd say. I have made a few observations about your charts below. Monthly I largely agree with your views, but am also not attached to the idea, as anything can happen at anytime. That overhead target could take some months (hehe) to get there. Weekly I typed up a rant about fakeouts but I decided to delete it. I urge you and others to use that term very sparingly and focus on what is real and happening in front of you each and every tick. Price follows the path of least resistance. You have outlined the possible resistance areas perfectly well. Your M5 chart has great confluence with your weekly analysis and your H4 analysis, so great job there. As I type I can see that there is definitely some selling pressure here, but how far it will go, is a mystery. How long you hold your short trade will come down to your pain tolerance and how long you're able to wait, but as the market can do anything at anytime, and make new decisions that were not part of an original analysis, it is important to take profit of the table at logical areas on the chart. I am not particularly convinced that price will head to the lower weekly target before the overhead target and visa versa, and I don't think the market has decided on that yet either, hence why we have had weeks and months of ranging. Let's put it this way, the long term charts are useful to put our shorter term trades into some https://www.readthemarket.com/index.php/en/forum/journals/2126-robbie-s-journal-observations?start=200 29/47 7/27/2018 Robbie's Journal & Observations - Page 9 - RTM Community overall context, they are much much harder to use as long term target practice if you're HOME EDUCATION FORUM MORE LOGOUT not the patient type. New decisions have to be made. H4 Yes, sure, it was an engulf at one point in time, but the whole move that caused the engulf, has been engulfed itself. Pay credence to the new information on the chart, which contradicts your original views. There are many engulfs on a chart that are no longer 'useful' engulfs, as a chart evolves. You will have to discover this for yourself over many laborious months of charting M5 I like the idea of a touch trade on the upper blue box for entry and the stop above the lower red line. Maximise that risk reward A strategy, if you get stopped out, is to wait for bearish PA on the M5 chart within the entire M30 zone to signal you back in. (For what it's worth your analysis sent some pips my way, I took the trade.) In summary Because I've crapped on a bit, some things to be mindful of: 1. The word 'fake' often comes with negative connotations and thus 'fakeout' can be harmful, be very careful of the language and terms you use to describe your trading as depending on how you have a term defined in your mind, it will affect how you take action and execute your trading plan. 2. An analysis of a chart in one moment of time will not always be relevant by the time you make the trade. A good analysis requires constant information gathering using multiple time frames and from pre-trade to post-trade.
